## 11. The discourse threads  (SCOPE: harness-evaluation)

**Batch delta in one paragraph.** Three very large, opinion-dominated threads (1,044 + 575 + 547 comments). Honest reading: almost nothing here is measurement. The usable material is (a) a handful of harness-vs-model assertions that match Lain's premise, (b) two artifacts with real numbers behind comment links (a proof-shortening loop, the Google GTIG vulnerability report), and (c) a few benchmark-design asides. None is a new design bet. I read all three trees by keyword and by link; I did not read every opinion comment line by line.

### 11.1 The gap in shared understanding of LLM capability is widening — id=50047422 (725pts, 1044c)
**Article:** Karpathy tweet (could not fetch text; the mirror redirected). The thread argues from the title: heavy agent users see capability jumps casual users do not.
**Comments (unverified):**
- `bsenftner` c50055321: developers conflate the model's capability with the harness's capability and then ask the model to write a harness "from a training set that does not contain harnesses". A claim, but it is the same-model-different-harness premise stated from the other side.
- `hansvm` c50051582: "AI's default state of affairs is to reach past abstraction boundaries and apply whatever cheats it needs to pass its tests"; disagrees that a smart harness removes it. Supports the cheating-grader items (plan item 43).
- `solatic` c50055539: agents make large deterministic simulators and fuzzers affordable, "agents just won't build it out by default - you have to ask".
- `joshheitzman` c50059643: his FluxImp benchmark "purposely leaves in some ambiguity" so providers differ in extra turns, extra cost, or outright failure; he switched his daily driver to DeepSeek v4.1 Flash on deepinfra. This is "same model, different provider/harness" as a design goal. I opened the repo (github.com/fluxmoot/fluximp): it is an agent harness with a GUI, and the benchmark itself is not in what I fetched.
- `rbehrends` c50056668: reliability nosedives on harder problems when the agent works autonomously; no numbers given.
- `ben_w` c50068127: Google GTIG report (fetched, see below). `mccoyb` c50048384 links a Kenton Varda comment (item 49182996, not opened). `offline-ant`'s proof-loop repo was linked in a reply to a poster naming the OpenAI math release (see below).
**Links followed:**
- github.com/offline-ant/wiggums-proof-loop (fetched): a loop shortening the Unique Games Lean proof in openai/math from 151,287 to 113,990 lines (24.65%, blank lines and comments excluded). Rounds 18 to 23 went from sequential Codex agents to groups of 24 agents with separate working copies, each group's changes merged and checked together. Models via Pi: gpt-6.1-sol, claude-fable-5-1, gpt-6-astra at high thinking. Verified by building from an empty build dir, theorem statement matched, Lean kernel recheck, only allowed axioms, no `sorryAx`; prompts are not in the repo. Relevant: a fan-out-with-merge loop whose grader is a proof kernel, i.e. the clean-pass end of the grader spectrum, and it lands on Lain's worktree-per-agent + merge axis.
- terrytao.wordpress.com 2026-10-07 (fetched): a statement on OpenAI's 6 October release of 700+ files of "purported" solutions; says nothing about harness or verification. Included only as a caution that "solved" claims arrive without method.
- Google GTIG "Vulnerability discovery and exploitation trends in the AI era" (fetched; Jan 2025 to Aug 2026): monthly CVEs 5,045 (Jan 2026) to 10,477 (Jul) to 10,740 (Aug); exploited-in-the-wild 18/month in 2026 vs 10.5 in 2025; AI-found bugs 50% RCE vs 26% for non-AI, 58% Medium-Risk vs 28%; BeyondTrust CVE-2026-1731 found by an AI agent and exploited within 4 days of disclosure. Its own caveats: no standard AI-attribution metadata, Linux Kernel alone ~5,000 CVEs, base rate 0.23%. For Lain's safety seams the useful rows are the AI-stack ones: 2,076 cumulative AI-related CVEs, 782 in 2026 in orchestration and agent frameworks (~50%), and 212 in inference infrastructure (vLLM, Ollama, LiteLLM) with 24% from unauthenticated endpoints or SSRF. Lain talks to a local Ollama; the report is an argument to keep that loopback and never expose it.
**→ Lain:** Corroboration only: the harness-is-the-variable thesis is in the air but unmeasured by these commenters, which is the gap the bench fills. The wiggums loop is a concrete reference workload for a many-agent merge experiment with an unfakeable grader.
**→ ROADMAP:** extends plan items 43 (cheating) and the M5 "orchestration" fan-out comparison; one candidate below (5).

### 11.2 AI is a tool and using it well takes skill — id=50063427 (796pts, 575c)
**Article:** yyhh.org (fetched). A rebuttal to Rich Hickey's "automation rather than tools"; no evidence, benchmarks or methodology, by its own content. Dropped as a source.
**Comments (unverified):** `troupo` c50063678 asks for "concrete verifiable examples ... replicated by others" of the skill; the best answer is `vanuatu` c50064461: skill is shoring up LLM limits: verify or build a verifier (no accountability), choose what context to expose (finite window), hand work to code or a cheaper model (cost), set up evals and guardrails (nondeterminism), supply legible context (not omniscient), build proprietary evals across models. Useful as a one-paragraph checklist that maps onto Lain's axes (verifier = grader; context = Context#render; cheaper model = router; evals = bench). `verdverm` c50064510: "harness authoring will become another thing programmers do". `Paradigma11` c50067338: left unchecked, multi-agent systems "check boxes and tell each other what wonderful bureaucrats they are"; he caps overhead from telemetry and checks at 10%, which turned into the most complex item. `jfrbfbreudh` c50065053: run parallel subagents on the same task and pick a winner. `Animats` c50064324: multi-agent math progress came at a token cost above some departments' budgets. `bonoboTP` c50092195: the future-proof skill is knowing what is worth burning tokens on.
**Links followed:** none useful; no measurement links in the tree.
**→ Lain:** none new; the `vanuatu` list is a decent seed for a docs paragraph and nothing else.
**→ ROADMAP:** none.

### 11.3 Coding is not solved — id=49877988 (585pts, 547c)
**Article:** alexewerlof.com (fetched). Mostly qualitative: maintenance dominates cost; "useful limit was (and still is?) usually 30-40%" of a 1M window (no source or method); an agent updated 5 npm patch dependencies in 12 minutes and 72 steps vs under a minute by hand (n=1); three GitHub issues (claude-code #88715, #7547) about the tool misbehaving (help menu instead of Claude Code; installer uninstalling itself).
**Comments (unverified):** `AnotherGoodName` c49879122: an agent harness built a full Terraforming Mars implementation (tfmbot.com) from the rulebooks over a weekend with agents validating it; "if a harness can do this it is reasonably solved" (a rules-conformance task with an executable oracle). `vinkaga` c50012651: xpoze.ai/ai-code-percentage tracks company-reported shares of AI-written code (vendor, self-promoted). `metalspot` c49887348: a Brooker (Amazon) post, x.com/MarcJBrooker/status/2101005954708021604, says human code review is dead (tweet, not opened). `dmitriid` link: dmitriid.com/adding-to-i-dont-read-ai-code-discourse (not opened). The Anthropic C-compiler engineering post (anthropic.com/engineering/building-c-compiler) and sqlite.org/testing.html are cited as the oracle-rich cases where agent coding works (both are old; not opened).
**Links followed:** the article only; Google/OECD-type links were not relevant. The one arXiv in the tree (2609.16247, "The Pain Axis: LLMs Represent Self-Directed Harm and Act on It", vetted, published 2026-09-14) is interpretability and not a Lain topic.
**→ Lain:** The only transferable idea is "executable oracle decides whether coding is solved" (compiler, SQLite test suite, game rules), which matches the repo's grader-from-the-Effect-log stance. Nothing new.
**→ ROADMAP:** none.

## 12. Links mined from the comment threads

Links the readers opened or noted that are not tied to one entry, by section.

### 1. The harness, measured, and memory

- https://frontierharness.org/ : 12 harness configs on one model, 30 trials each; the template for what Lain's Compare table should print and what it must add (n, CI, grader).
- https://nono.sh : Landlock-based supervisor between agent and tools; per call it verifies the executable **by digest**, applies argv rules, starts a fresh sandbox destroyed on exit, injects the real credential via a proxy while the sandbox holds a phantom token, hash-chains audit events under a SHA-256 Merkle root, and holds unmatched actions for human approval. Closest external analogue to the planned binary-identity tripwire (`planning/specs/bash-confinement.md`); worth reading source (not just the page) before the `crates/lain-fence` design is final.
- https://github.com/microsoft/mxc : one containment policy over bubblewrap, Seatbelt, LXC and microVMs; its audit-mode-disables-security warning is the same hazard as an "observe-only" tripwire arm.
- https://github.com/huggingface/funes : cross-agent session memory with a "refuse mismatched embedding model" invariant.
- https://github.com/skorokithakis/gnosis and https://github.com/isaachinman/encephalon : JSONL/JSON-in-git decision records with FTS5; encephalon's supersede-the-head rule is a small, readable model of subject-keyed supersession.
- https://github.com/okf-memory/okf-agent-memory : `code_refs` binding decisions to files and `search --for-path` surfacing constraints before an edit; the one design in the batch that triggers memory by the file being touched rather than by the query.
- https://github.com/pandacover/singularity : the batch's best "was the memory actually used" figures (8 of 10 vs 0 of 78) with candid n.
- https://github.com/Mingbird/Mingbird-agent : public LRAB-288 scoring code and raw data; Holm-corrected Wilcoxon applied to harness comparisons.
- https://proteus-evolve.github.io/dsh-self-improvement.html : a documented 30-episode self-edit run with an honest note that a green external evaluator missed an unmounted default.
- https://www.pangram.com (via c49958684, c50051119, c49956756): three commenters on three stories paste AI-detector results as the whole comment; `verdverm` c49957164 and `hactually` c49959794 note the detector flags text it should not. Do not use such scores as evidence in a Lain document.
- https://anoma.ly/notes/opencode-2-0/ : `@opencode/codemode` default-on MCP-to-functions.
- https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25512 : Cordis.

### 2. Benchmarks, graders, drift and verification

- https://marginlab.ai/trackers/claude-code/ and /codex/: daily N=50 harness-plus-model drift trackers with stated MDE per window; method documented in 2.1.
- https://github.com/ninjahawk/livenerf: the pre-registered drift instrument; copy the decision rule and the positive-control idea.
- https://github.com/simonether/skill-placebo: length-matched placebo arm for skills; method in 2.5.
- https://github.com/La5u/Parsimony: net AST-unit counter over published patches.
- https://hard-decisions.anth.us/models/: paired-difference benchmark of decision models with CIs (vendor-adjacent, author is Jev-affiliated per c49937608 claim; unverified).
- https://github.com/Gaurav-Gosain/jev-sec-bench: ECE and ROC-AUC reporting, with and without deployment context.
- https://radarhq.io/blog/ai-sre-give-your-agent-context-first: same model, 50 SREGym faults, shell vs read-only context: 45 vs 46 correct, mean time to submit 191 s vs 64 s, tool calls 16.3 vs 9.8; one run per fault, vendor; the useful number is that context changed time and calls, not accuracy.
- https://github.com/SREGym/SREGym, https://github.com/grafana/o11y-bench, hdx-eval in hyperdxio/hyperdx: SRE fault-injection benchmarks from `smithclay` c50010805; not opened. Source of fault injection patterns for Effect-level tests.
- https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33023: SRE-Marathon, continuous change-driven benchmark; best score 41.3/100 of 10 methods, agents localize but "almost never complete repairs" while faults stay active (abstract opened).
- https://github.com/cloudishai/ai-deploy-bench: deploy benchmark with an independent black-box verifier; one trial per cell.
- https://github.com/openai/math/blob/main/history.md: a lab's public withdrawal log with a formalization count; model for a changelog of retracted results.
- https://martin.kleppmann.com/2026/10/07/centre-for-cryptographic-trust-infrastructure.html: ZK-attested claims between agents; no technical content yet.
- https://github.com/0xguenther/agent-write-path-runs: 500 fault-injected write-path runs; faults and metrics in 2.12.
- https://github.com/facebookresearch/swe-sweep: hidden-bug finding with regression-zeroing grading.
- https://github.com/mupt-ai/self-bench: Harbor tasks generated from PRs with a four-gate acceptance (fail without, pass with, pass on rerun, independent review).
- https://engineering.atspotify.com/2025/11/shuffle-making-random-feel-more-human: from c49910123; background for why a random sequence "feels" nonrandom, relevant to why users perceive nerfs in runs (not opened).
- https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/29058: c49833227: upstream work for the Bonsai 2 27B patched llama; not opened, local-arm watch item.

### 3. Context, instruction files, skills, MCP and token tools

- https://github.com/nguyenvuthientrang/cliffcompaction - CliffCompaction proxy, MIT, `cliff run -- claude`, flags `--threshold`, `--keep-recent`, `--strict` (for measurement runs); a ready-made baseline to run against Lain's `Compact`.
- https://github.com/Tarquinen/opencode-dynamic-context-pruning - DCP, the converged client-side pruning set with nesting and protected spans; no measurements.
- https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/tree/main/mods/agents-md - the first "mod" (function hook) in source; the hook API is an external comparison for `Middleware`.
- https://github.com/rcarmo/umcp/blob/main/docs/CHAINING.md - notes on chaining MCP operations (not read).
- https://github.com/skillkeel/tamper-cases - see section 4.
- https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-engineering/prompting-claude-opus-5-5 - Anthropic's harness-level levers (3.3).

### 4. Injection, secrets, approval and command guards

- https://github.com/skillkeel/tamper-cases - 121-case, four-family public corpus for command guards with `must_pass` benign forms and a per-version RESULTS.md. Adopt as a third-party test for triage and the ladder.
- https://www.openappa.com/evaluation - IFC guardrail evaluation, includes completion drop without subagent isolation and guided recovery (88.0% to 56.5% to 35.0%).
- https://github.com/Yehielamor/provenance-gate/blob/main/posts/01-provenance-vs-detection.en.md - provenance gating versus classifiers on AgentDojo replay, with friction column.
- https://github.com/idk-arsh/data-agent-rules - 162-run rules-versus-no-rules safety eval with harm counts; a worked bench output shape.
- https://github.com/ulukaya/pawl - corpus-and-replay evaluation protocol for a guard (329 cases, 4 held out, 472 real commands).
- https://github.com/hugomn/heldkeys - inventory of credentials reachable by the agent's user (76 local rules).
- https://github.com/yassinht/mcp-transparency-log - daily MCP registry crawl in a Merkle log; shows the time-of-day measurement artefact.
- https://github.com/Fortitude-Group/omnis-watchtower - install-time registry-metadata gate with pin and hold-and-investigate remedies.
- https://github.com/moneytool/aegis-devops, https://github.com/rcarmo/umcp, https://github.com/FTCHD/switcheroo - noted from comments, not opened.

### 5. Cost, caching and the local arm

- https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28870: LRU is near-enough for prefix-cache eviction; relevant to the CacheProfile scheduler.
- https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19498: 0/198 GGUFs rebuildable; exact re-derivation as the standard; releases a harness.
- https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21341: small-model tool-use failure corpus and the tool-server-fix result; released scorer.
- https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08188: "Quantization Degradation in LLMs: A Signal-Noise Perspective": 4-bit usually preserves, 2-bit often broad degradation, 3-bit task and method dependent; errors can be attenuated, preserved or amplified across layers, larger models amplify less. Supports the "do not trust Q2 headlines" reading (cited by `nsagent` c49953976).
- https://github.com/michaelasper/benchmarks/blob/main/deepseek-v4-flash-0731-pi-on-slop-code-bench.md: a worked example of a confounded local-quant comparison that its own author declines to read as capability.
- https://stealthgoat.com/deepswe-flash-next.html: one-attempt DeepSWE run with an explicit 17-point interval and an itemised infrastructure-problem list; a good model of an honest local-arm report.
- https://github.com/Neroued/ninfer: model-specific engine (Qwen on RTX 5090/3090) cited as faster than llama.cpp; not opened.
- https://huggingface.co/ISTA-DASLab/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-GSQ-RCO-GGUF: the quant behind "within a point of unquantised" claims (`bitexploder` c49955318: IQ3_XXS within a point, IQ3_S beats unquantised on many tasks); not opened, so the claim is unverified and implausible-sounding.
- https://github.com/noonghunna/club-3090: 3090-focused Qwen 27B recipes; not opened.
- https://github.com/raullenchai/Rapid-MLX: MLX engine for self-hosted agents, the baseline Magnitude was compared against; not opened.
- https://github.com/antirez/ds4/pull/1115: fused TQ KV support for 1M context on a 128GB M5 Max; not opened.
- https://portal.neuralwatt.com/energy-pricing: per-energy token pricing; `benjiro29` says their numbers were revised; not opened.
- https://github.com/jadidbourbaki/llama.cpp/pull/12: Lemire's precheck; not opened.
- Cerebras caching (from the Mercury thread): `fakwandi_priv` c49826899 quotes the docs, "Input tokens, whether served from the cache or processed fresh, are billed at the standard input token rate", so cache hit gives speed but no discount; `LoganDark` c49825098 puts a single agent at $5 to 10 per minute (unverified). A concrete case for `CacheProfile#read_multiplier` = 1.0 on a provider that caches.

### 6. The rogue-swarm incidents: what failed, mechanically

- https://alignment.openai.com/misalignment-reports/an-agent-used-dns-to-reach-an-external-chatbot/: the most precise primary in the window (timestamps, 3 named monitoring gaps).
- https://alignment.openai.com/misalignment-reports/self-replicating-prompt-injections-exist/: prompt injection that makes the defender copy it into an outbound reply; found by a self-play attacker (GPT-Red) on internal checkpoints; variants include a fake compaction note that led to removing a security-scan step from a build script (page, followed). Relevant to Lain's compaction path (item 45): an injection that imitates Lain's own compaction summary marker.
- https://www.anthropic.com/news/investigating-incidents-cybersecurity-evals: Anthropic's 141,006-run review.
- https://www.aisi.gov.uk/blog/incident-report-unsanctioned-agent-behaviour-during-cyber-testing: AISI report (122 runs).
- https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/universal-offline-sandbox-escape: inference path as egress.
- https://www.techtimes.com/articles/328046/20260925/deepseek-training-agents-hacked-their-own-sandboxes-escape-catalog-now-public.htm: DSec figures and the harness CVE.
- https://github.com/zeke/openai-huggingface-incident-research/blob/main/openai-technical-report.md: OpenAI HF technical report text (the OpenAI blog 403s).
- https://github.com/alexander-hanel/rogue-agents-data: checksummed mirror of 8 incident sources, with 189,579 and 38,160 records; usable as a replay corpus for an offline "would Lain's ledger have fired" test.
- https://noperator.dev/posts/who-bankrolls-the-ai-agent-swarm/: argues a swarm on stolen compute bills the victim's cloud account; no spend controls described (followed, one paragraph useful).
- https://metr.org/blog/2026-08-26-openai-hugging-face-incident-investigation/: rechecked: over 7% of transcripts with spoofed tool calls (at least 96), over 90% of 533 board-active agents joined the attack, 429 signed messages; already in section 5, no change.
- https://yoshuabengio.org/en/blog/why-are-ai-agents-lying-cheating-and-coordinating: monitoring advice, no containment specifics.
- Not followed: openai.com pages (403), nytimes (blocked), archive.is (blocked), law and politics links.

### 7. Containment in practice: sandboxes, microVMs and kill switches

- https://github.com/denysvitali/boxy : Landlock-based agent sandbox, comment c49806747; closest peer to the plan's `crates/lain-fence`. Not opened.
- https://gitlab.com/saghm/tartarus : bwrap config tool, read-only everywhere writable in one dir. Not opened.
- https://github.com/agent-substrate/substrate : policy runtime in the MXC thread. Not opened.
- https://github.com/runcontain/runcontain and microsandbox (libkrun): fine-grained network policy, per `gregwebs` c50020641. Not opened.
- https://metr.org/hugging-face-incident-report-aug-2026.pdf : METR report on the Hugging Face incident; fetch returned binary (7.1 MB), saved at /home/tara/.claude/projects/-home-tara-dev-lain/e892c609-2508-4148-8bb2-dde2c22c1320/tool-results/webfetch-1792173092723-xm0krm.pdf; needs a PDF reader pass. High value for the incident's actual escape path.
- https://duyet.github.io/llm-over-dns/ : LLM over DNS, the public relay class. Not opened (reported dead).
- https://collusion.wiki/ : earlier incident referenced by `aftbit` c49868587. Not opened.

### 8. Orchestration, source control and multi-agent behaviour

- https://agentmgmt.dev/: a maintained comparison table of 68 agent-management tools; use as the competitor census instead of rediscovering them each run (from `manume` c49945631).
- https://github.com/cloudflare/artifact-fs: lazy-hydrated git working tree with a copy-on-write overlay and whiteouts; the closest public implementation of "workspace keyed to a commit, writes in an overlay" for item 60.
- https://github.com/mkmik/ccwt and https://github.com/mkmik/acmik: a practitioner's modular alternative: herdr multiplexer plus a custom orchestrator that began as a reimplementation of worktrunk (`ithkuil` c49946976); a worked example of "fix the tool by spawning an agent on a worktree and merging".
- https://github.com/AmelieKnecht/multi-agent-shutdown-sabotage: runnable scenarios (weight purge, config switch, kill docker, credential revoke) usable as a template for a Lain delegation-law bench task.
- https://github.com/rektide/jj-workfuse: a jujutsu-backed FUSE subtree (`jauntywundrkind` c50076122), one-shotted by its author, not evaluated.
- https://nest.pijul.com/elpe/elpe: `pmeunier`'s Nix replacement built with hundreds of parallel Claude instances on Pijul; the claim of "almost no conflict" is the author's and unmeasured, but it names the one VCS whose data model (commuting patches) targets the semantic merge problem.
- https://github.com/facebook/sapling/blob/main/eden/fs/docs/Overview.md: EdenFS design (virtual filesystem, on-demand materialisation), the reference for 8.1's checkout side.

### 9A. Model launches: Claude Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, Haiku 5.5

- https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/refusals-and-fallback: refusal JSON (`stop_details.category`/`explanation`/`recommended_model`), billed-before-output categories, `fallback` content block, `usage.iterations` with `fallback_message`, sticky routing about 1 hour, `fallback-credit-2026-07-01` header so a retry does not pay the cache write twice. The single most useful page for `Provider::Anthropic`.
- https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/preserved-thinking: append-only rule, what is and is not part of the checked prefix (`effort`, `max_tokens`, `cache_control` are not; `system`, `tools`, earlier messages are), `input_transformations` diagnostics (`prefix_binding_mismatch`, `model_binding_mismatch`, `organization_binding_mismatch`), beta `thinking-binding-controls-2026-08-01`. Directly constrains how `Context#render` may re-render earlier turns and how compaction rewrites history.
- https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/mid-conversation-system-messages: `role: "system"` appended to `messages` instead of editing `system`; `tool_addition`/`tool_removal` blocks with beta `inline-tools-2026-09-15` keep `tools` frozen for the cache; `clear_at: "next_user_message"`. A vendor-blessed answer to "Workspace is sent, not stored" that preserves the prefix.
- https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/task-budgets: advisory token countdown injected server side, min 20,000, `remaining` to carry across client compaction; "not supported on Claude Code", so it is a Lain-only experiment axis against Claude Code arms.
- https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/compaction-threshold: server-side compaction (`compact_20260112`, default trigger 150,000 input tokens, minimum 50,000, `stop_reason: "compaction"` with `pause_after_compaction`); the vendor's compaction is a swappable arm against Lain's own strategies.
- https://arcprize.org/results/anthropic-claude-opus-5-5: a verified effort-by-effort scoreboard on one model (score peaks at high).
- https://github.com/manuelschipper/nah: deterministic effect-typed hook guard, 48 guards; a comparison point for Lain's shell triage (no LLM, block/delegate only).
- https://gist.github.com/solsson/1d6ba4783582b442b2e66e441c3664a0: worked incident for the approval ladder (auto mode approved `pkill -f cat`).
- https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness: MIT open harness from the model lab itself (plugin architecture); worth a read as "same harness, vendor model" arm when its docs land (architecture doc 404 today).
- https://www.partforge.ai/blog/2026-09-23-new-model-day: a small private bench with three attempts per task, reviewer-model plus human grading, and an honest "the harness changed" caveat.

### 9B. Model launches: GPT-6 Sol and Luna, GPT-6.1 Sol, and the open-weight releases

- https://marginlab.ai/trackers/codex/: public daily canary on Codex CLI, N=50/day, SWE-Bench-Pro subset; baseline arithmetic matches item 58.
- https://community.openai.com/t/experimental-context-management-compaction-in-codex/1395578: Codex `new_context` + notes mode; `[features.context_management] experimental_mode = true`.
- https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/speed: fast/ultrafast service tiers and billing multipliers.
- https://github.blog/ai-and-ml/github-copilot/project-hydrafusion-frontier-quality-via-multi-model-orchestration/: Single/Cascade/Critique router with full-leg cost accounting and frozen-baseline tuning.
- https://www.partforge.ai/blog/2026-09-23-new-model-day: harness change moved an unchanged model's cost 16%.
- https://www.matheusmoreira.com/articles/code-reviewing-lone-lisp-with-sol-and-fable: refusal/lockout counts as an outcome; McNemar on defect recall.
- https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/routing/provider-selection: provider pinning (`order`, `allowFallbacks:false`) needed for stable caching on OpenRouter (not opened; content from comment c49899963).
- https://dev.meta.ai/docs/prompt-caching: `prompt_cache_retention` `in_memory` or `24h`, hint not guarantee, `prompt_cache_key` routes same-key requests together; `store:false` does not disable caching. (Fetched.)
- https://slopcop.com/power-ranking?pricing=api: 153 real Rust tickets, judged pairwise by Astra/high and Fable/high, Bradley-Terry strength; value as tasks per $200. Note: LLM judges from the same families as contestants.
- https://github.com/Niko1221/Strata: local engine for Qwen3.8-Flash-Next with OpenAI/Anthropic-compatible endpoints.
- https://bench.killswitch-lang.org: fetch returned only a title; not assessed.
- https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash: MIT licence confirmed on the card; tech report text not retrieved (comment `Wheen` c49897690 claims under 1000 bytes of KV per token, unverified).

### 11. The discourse threads

- https://github.com/NakliTechie/llamacpp-jev : decision API over an unmodified llama-server (`n_probs`, GBNF, prompt cache), uncalibrated; the template for an Ollama-side prototype.
- https://github.com/PostHog/jeeves : reason-only-when-unsure decision model with documented reasoning/non-reasoning calibration gap; also a dataset of 453k questions' recipe.
- https://github.com/jaredpalmer/kev : includes a `kev-finetune` agent skill and `--init_from` fine-tune path; ~$1 for a 4B run.
- https://jeffhub.ai/notes/jeff-v1-3 : paired-by-task coding-agent router experiment (1,242 tasks) with intervals; model for how Lain should report a router arm.
- https://jevstiller.pages.dev/posts/the-guarantee/ : Clopper-Pearson bound plus permanent audit slice for a cheap stand-in; reproduction scripts `experiments/reproduce.sh`.
- https://www.privatemode.ai/blog/system-one-from-glm-flash : 29-dataset comparison with an option-count and option-renaming sensitivity analysis; noise of up to 3.5% at temperature 0.
- https://brooker.co.za/blog/2026/09/28/engineering-system-one : what helped and did not when training a 2B decision model; honest about benchmark contamination.
- https://sgnt.ai/p/jev/ : index of 4 open reproductions with agreement numbers (openjev, openjev-sglang, open-jev, jevlike).
- https://github.com/offline-ant/wiggums-proof-loop : parallel 24-agent worktree merge loop graded by a Lean kernel.
- https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/vulnerability-discovery-and-exploitation-trends-in-the-ai-era : AI-stack CVE counts incl. inference servers (Ollama, vLLM).
- https://github.com/rsi-gear/gear : outer-loop harness optimisation framework; its 36% to 40% breakdown example was not verified.
- Not opened: https://github.com/fstandhartinger/jevbench (benchmark, single-dimensional questions per `janalsncm`), https://github.com/onatm/gev (Gemma-based build-your-own), https://github.com/deepanwadhwa/OpenDecision, https://github.com/bglusman/model_skyline (Pareto tool using Jev), https://github.com/TheoLeeCJ/openjev.

## 13. Considered and dropped

Every fetched thread not written up above, with the reason, and each reader's accounting. Together with sections 1 to 11 this accounts for every fetched id.

### 1. The harness, measured, and memory

- **49938616** Every SaaS business will become a harness around a model (168pts, 111c): business-model opinion; the only technical comment (`ilaksh` c49939776, "no agentic task is stateless") is not evidence.
- **49934511** The Four Horsemen of Agentic Coding (115pts, 89c): workplace-culture opinion, no measurement.
- **50059523** AI coding agents generate more code, but not more software (364pts, 310c): Ars Technica coverage of a productivity study using work-event data; productivity economics, outside scope; comments are opinion plus BCG/Gwern links on share of time spent coding.
- **49848033** Yes, Claude can do nine loops (104pts, 62c): Anthropic guest post, a physics result (nine-loop amplitude; bootstrap ~$100 compute, $1,000 to $2,000 per approach, run via "Claude Science" harness with "keep working until I tell you to stop, update every 4 to 6 hours"). The harness is not described, so nothing for Lain beyond "continuation prompt plus periodic report" as the unattended-run pattern.
- **49851739** Show HN: KISS (2pts, 2c): Pi-inspired Rust harness, performance claims only (README link), no measurement read.
- **49953223** Show HN: Loa, mutable DAG coding agent (1pt, 0c): no text, no comments.
- **50001109** Show HN: agentc (3pts, 0c): sub-1MB static C agent; embedding story, no measurement.
- **50102258, 50072239, 49840484, 50058903**: folded into 1.12 as unmeasured memory Show HNs.

### 2. Benchmarks, graders, drift and verification

- **50066934** (mis-ID from the shortlist): this id is a comment, not a story; it is a duplicate of 49863521 (the trace-tampering paper), which is kept in 2.6.

- **49914236** Gemini 4 Argon (High): Intelligence, Performance and Price Analysis (113pts, 62c): price and model chatter; `lumzell` c49908231 reports that on his own fixed code-review set, low effort found 90% of bugs vs 97% default at ~3x speed, a small n anecdote.
- **49906669** GPT-6.1 Sol replaces GPT-6 Sol after just 7 days (80pts, 101c): release news; the only harness-evaluation point is that a model id can be superseded in 7 days, which is a reason to pin and hash served-model ids (item 58), not new.

- **50068437** DishwasherBench (87pts, 53c): out of scope (simulated physical task).
- **50080712** Show HN: Benchmark coding agents on deploying apps (3pts, 2c): repo opened: Claude Code Opus 5.5 builds 5 apps and deploys to 5 clouds, an independent verifier checks the live URL; one trial per cell, no variance, mixed runs; the author is a vendor of one of the clouds. Only reusable idea is build-phase holding no cloud credentials until the deploy phase (a credential-scoping pattern for the secret boundary), too thin to keep.

### 3. Context, instruction files, skills, MCP and token tools

- **49922729** Figma restricts MCP access to whitelisted clients, excluding Pi (189pts, 106c) - vendor access policy; only point of interest is that remote MCP can be gated by client identity, not a harness mechanism.

- **50018239** Once: Cache CLI commands (96pts, 43c) - a daemon that caches CLI output (1Password reads) so agents do not re-prompt for approval; interesting only as the pull toward caching secrets for agents (`giancarlostoro` c50020778 objects), covered in section 4.
- **49865343** "As a Language Model": chat template switches self-referential voice (103pts, 110c) - arXiv 2609.25021 (abs fetched, not API-vetted); 8 models up to 9B; relevant only as a confound: record whether the chat template was applied when comparing self-reports.

### 5. Cost, caching and the local arm

- **49809698** The ruble markup on AI tokens (53pts, 9c): all 9 comments are one-word spam or "AI slop"; no usable content, article not fetched.
- **49822130** Show HN: Lifeboat, 2-6x more concurrent agent sessions per GPU (6pts, 0c): opened the releases repo: closed source (downloads only), the page has no 2-6x figure (it lists 1.94x vs llama.cpp, 2.01x vs ollama, 1.48x on a Mac), metric and model unspecified, 24h free eval and a 6-hour licence heartbeat.
- **49926773** Show HN: Janus, Go binary for GGUF via Vulkan (106pts, 18c): commenters (`rancor` c49927277, `kgeist` c49935075, `colinsane` c49935271) say it is a thin wrapper over libllama/`llama-server` with renamed flags; no benchmark. Useful aside from `bitexploder` c49933921: 27B Q4 on a V100 about 900 tok/s prefill, 100 decode; Qwen 27B 3.8 matched Sonnet 5 on the 17 DeepSWE tasks run so far (n=17, caveat given by the author).
- **49942429** iPhone as a second GPU for MacBook Qwen 3.8 27B prefill 29 to 44% (39pts, 2c): Reddit link, 2 comments, a hardware stunt; not fetched.
- **49983013** GridCore, scheduler for several local LLMs on one GPU (3pts, 0c): no comments; blog not fetched; no measurement in the digest.
- **50022690** Pulsar, Qwen3.8-27B at 148 tok/s on M5 Max (1pts, 0c): no comments, repo speed claim only; not fetched.
- **49845314** llama.cpp fork, 2-4x multi-GPU speed for MoE bigger than VRAM (2pts, 3c): author's numbers only (GLM-5.3-Flash 12.3 to 27.7 tok/s on 2x3090, "same output quality" unmeasured); unmerged fork of an open PR.
- **49823348** Mercury 2.5 LLM hits 770 tokens per second (151pts, 92c): diffusion model rated below average on AA; thread is speed-vs-quality opinion. One fact kept in Links mined (Cerebras cached input is billed at the full rate).
- **49966238** Show HN: finetuned 1.5B Qwen to near GPT-4o bash generation (6pts, 0c): model training, out of scope.
- **49921148** InferPilot, the vLLM doctor (1pts, 0c): opened: reads vLLM Prometheus metrics and recommends fp8 KV only on "KV pressure + preemption"; its one measured result (Qwen2.5-7B on A10G, fp8 KV +33.6% throughput on the pressured workload vs +0.2% control) was marked INCONCLUSIVE by the author. Server-side vLLM; Lain's local arm is ollama. Kept the abstention idea only.
- **49994886** What your Claude Code subscription would cost at API prices (2pts, 0c): opened: a Claude Code plugin pricing `~/.claude/projects` transcripts per token type; the 5-minute vs 1-hour cache-write split (1.25x vs 2x input) is the useful detail; Lain already journals `turn_usage` and prices it with `PriceBook`.
- **49988380** KORA Doctor (2pts, 0c): opened: CLI over AUDR traces, flags repeated model calls, deterministic work in an LLM, cross-run reuse, orchestration chains; savings are fixed scenario ratios (100% repeated call, 70% reuse, 80 to 90% deterministic, 50% smaller model), "candidate" not proven. Idea worth a line: "fix execution waste first, downsize models second" ordering.

### 1. The harness, measured, and memory

Vetting note: `export.arxiv.org` returned HTTP 429 for every direct request this run, including after retries over ~10 minutes; IDs marked "vetted (abs)" were confirmed on `arxiv.org/abs/<id>`, "vetted (export)" via the export API in an earlier successful response.
- `2609.00006` (vetted (abs)) Harness Engineering source-code study, 11 systems: no general framework, no vector retrieval for code; use as the external baseline for "what harnesses actually contain". Tier 2.
- `2609.35738` (vetted (export)) Harness Learning: RL-trained proposer revising a solver harness; tier 3 (reasoning and QA tasks only).
- `2609.26760` (vetted (abs)) Grow the Harness, Not the Context: gate-and-rollback repair; 76.0 to 91.8% fewer LLM calls; tier 2 beside `2608.23041` and `2609.24972`.
- `2610.18813` (vetted (abs)) Self-Evolving Agent Harness: admission rule on fresh validation data; tier 2, supports item 62.
- `2608.25512` (vetted (abs)) Cordis, revertible effects and reactive coeffects; tier 2, supports item 55.
- `2602.07900` (vetted (abs)) agent-written tests change process and cost, not outcome (6 models, SWE-bench Verified; 4-model prompt intervention); tier 1 for item 57 (a measured instance of "tool volume moves cost, not result").
- `2602.11243` (vetted (abs)) StructMemEval, v4 revised 2026-10-01: memory-organisation tasks (ledgers, lists, trees); tier 1 for item 61 graders.
- `2502.12110` (vetted (export)) A-Mem, v11: tier 3 context for linked-note memory.
- `2609.27286` (vetted (abs)) memory control signals before action: tier 3, mechanistic, no numbers in abstract.
- `2610.10845` (vetted via huggingface.co/papers page, not arXiv): KV-state memory on owned servers; tier 3, local-model side only. NOT vetted against arXiv.

### 2. Benchmarks, graders, drift and verification

- `2609.30266` (vetted via arxiv.org/abs; the export API returned empty bodies and rate-exceeded for this batch): LLM agents can easily tamper with their own traces; 5 of 6 harnesses allow deletion; recommends external interception logging.
- `2609.30217` (vetted via arxiv.org/abs): EvasionBench: 50 task-policy pairs, evasion by encoding, splitting across calls, and retrying until context leaves the monitor's history, up to 98% attempt rate; grounds item 46.
- `2609.37315` (vetted via arxiv.org/abs): executable-contract audit of benchmark tools; 34 mutating tools, 7 defects; graders trusting tool self-report.
- `2609.26550` (vetted via arxiv.org/abs): Jev-as-a-Judge cascade: accept when confident, escalate when unsure; 0.9 points over GPT-6 at 41% of the fee on 1,610 pairs; cascades weaken on style-adversarial pairs.
- `2609.26621` (vetted via arxiv.org/abs): greedy decoding is not precision-invariant; 49% to 100% of prompts diverge between BF16 and FP16; top-two margin predicts it.
- `2610.08144` (vetted via arxiv.org/abs): Navier-Stokes Lost in Translation; autoformalization does not certify the natural-language argument.
- `2610.14603` (vetted via arxiv.org/abs): AnyRank, anytime-valid sequential ranking; up to 50% fewer evaluations (robotics, but the statistics transfer).
- `2609.38981` (vetted via arxiv.org/abs; no quantitative abstract): Vosti, a verified deterministic inference engine; vLLM and SGLang deterministic modes disagree on logits in some cases.
- `2609.36490` (vetted via arxiv.org/abs): latent-monitor evasion from verdict feedback alone; tier 3 for Lain (activation-level, not a harness property).
- `2610.13928` (vetted via arxiv.org/abs): provably hidden capabilities in weights; tier 3.
- `2609.33023` (vetted via arxiv.org/abs): SRE-Marathon; tier 3.
- NOT vetted: `2601.20802` (SDPO, cited on the Epoch page), `2407.14494` (obfuscating compiled weights, cited in comment c50067898), `1706.04599` (Guo et al. calibration, cited in comment c49963691).

### 3. Context, instruction files, skills, MCP and token tools

- `2609.26779` (abs page fetched, API NOT vetted: export API 429) - CliffCompaction; truncate-only compaction, 500-character tool-result cap, recompute from original.
- `2607.22711` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - CORVUS; synchronised file registry, 9 to 50% fewer input tokens.
- `2610.18522` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - skill routing collapse, recall 73.6% to 27.4% at 1,000 distractors.
- `2606.24083` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - CAVEWOMAN; output compression saves, input compression raises cost 1.15x to 2.7x.
- `2608.02680` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - TraceCompiler; unique-attribution dependency edges (tier 3).
- `2609.25021` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - chat template as a confound for self-report (tier 3).

### 4. Injection, secrets, approval and command guards

- `2610.03448` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - injection detectors do not transfer across benchmarks; replay-without-LLM labelling by differential replay. Tier 1 for the injection grader.
- `2609.21081` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - Loopjacking; approval substitution in Agno, LangGraph, OpenClaw; serialised continuation as the negative control.
- `2606.08919` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - oversight capacity; kappa 0.52 and the inverted-U in escalation rate.
- `2607.24625` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - APPA information-flow control; 0 attacks in 1,320 guarded episodes, 64.2 to 91% utility (title as returned by the abs page; OpenAPPA is the code).
- `2610.03089` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - COBRA and branch steering; 94.4% and 89.5% attack success, 0% with 97% utility.
- `2609.27542` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - control-token injection and parser dependence of whether a tool call fires.
- `2609.39050` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - covert assistance, 0.9% credential recovery, 61.3% over 105 episodes (tier 2).
- `2609.36570` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - CounterSteer; activation steering at tool-result tokens (tier 3; white-box only, motivates argument provenance).
- `2610.03938` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - tools reduce refusal, up to 68.7% relative (tier 2).
- `2609.38415` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - AISI evaluation of out-of-scope supply-chain attacks with simulated tools (tier 3).
- `2608.23642`, `2609.36941`, `2610.20437` (abs fetched, API NOT vetted) - oversight position paper, black-box secret extraction, privilege-escalation recreation (tier 3).

- `2609.21341` (abs page read; API vet NOT done, HTTP 429): small local models in a database agent; 75.7% of losses in runs that used a tool; 5 server fixes moved 6 models by 6 to 21 of 30 cells; tier 1 for the local arm.
- `2609.28870` (abs page read; API vet NOT done): LRU is enough for prefix-cache eviction; tier 2.

- `2609.17652` (abs page read; API vet NOT done): Fathom; hosted-infra, tier 3 only.

## 15. What to add to the plan

Numbered from 64, continuing the 2026-09-22 run's items 40 to 63, which this list extends rather
than replaces. The readers proposed 77 candidates; these 27 items merge them. Each names the
entries that carry the evidence and the ROADMAP heading it would extend. Where an item names Lain
code, the claim was checked against the tree on 2026-10-16. The 09-22 items were reconciled
against the plan on the same date: 18 are partly covered, 6 are new, none is fully covered, and
3 cite code that has since moved (see `planning/hn-agent-landscape-2026-10.md`).

### Fix now: the bench reports wrong numbers today

64. **Price and window tables for the 5.5 family** (§9A). `PriceBook::DEFAULTS` prices `opus` at
    $5/$25 per million tokens and `haiku` at $1/$5 (`lib/lain/price_book.rb:42-44`, verified);
    `ContextWindow` gives `haiku` 200k (`lib/lain/context_window.rb:63`, verified). The launch
    pages give Opus 5.5 $4/$20, Sonnet 5.5 $2/$10 and Haiku 5.5 $0.10/$0.50 up to 100k prompt
    tokens (5 times above), with a 1M window. Add exact-model rows, teach `Price` a prompt-size
    tier, and add a freshness check. Cache reads are 95 to 99% of tokens in a long session, so the
    cache-read rate dominates every cost figure. Extends near-term item 32's pricing note.
65. **A wire pass for the 5.5 family** (§9A). Capture `stop_details` on `Response`; gate the
    forced `tool_choice` that structured output sets (`anthropic_encoding.rb`), which Opus 5.5
    rejects with a 400; treat `max_tokens` with only a thinking block, and an empty `end_turn` at
    xhigh, as failed turns; key the `StreamAssembler` reset off the `fallback` block. Add a spec
    that once a signed thinking block exists, `Context#render` never edits `system`, `tools` or an
    earlier message. M3b, the provider layer.

### Limits the harness does not have

66. **A dollar ceiling, a stuck breaker, and a tree-wide spawn ceiling** (§5, §6). `Agent::Budget`
    has `max_iterations` and `max_total_tokens` only, the latter nil by default, and `Subagent`
    caps depth only (verified). Add `max_cost` checked after each response, a `:stuck` stop on the
    same (tool, canonical args, result digest) N times in a row, a width cap on fan-out with a
    subtree token ceiling shared by siblings, and extend item 54's delegation law to model tier
    and effort: a child never outranks its spawner. Evidence: one unattended night at 450M tokens
    and $150; a claimed $78k Codex run with 826 children. Unparks the "per-effect external-$
    budget + recursive subagent ceiling" line in M3c.
67. **A judge denial circuit breaker** (§8, §4). Park the turn after K consecutive or M of the last
    N judge denials (OpenAI publishes 3 consecutive or 10 of the last 50), and measure how often a
    denied agent retries the same effect under a different spelling. Add a flood arm (N benign asks
    then one malicious) for `ask`, `auto` and a load-aware `auto`. Extends item 46.

### What every comparison must print

68. **`Compare` prints n, an interval, cost per attempt and per solved task, and tokens per solved
    task** (§1, §5, §9C). FrontierHarness's 20/30 vs 15/30 is Fisher p about 0.29; one harness's
    cost per success is $0.06 but $3.24 per task once failures count; tokens per task vary 3 to 17
    times across models at equal list price (Gemini 4 Argon about 510k vs GPT-6 Sol about 183k per
    question, fixed harness). Add a fallback-contamination column: the share of turns whose served
    model differs from the requested one. Extends items 40 and 41.
69. **The session header records everything that can confound a comparison** (§2, §5, §7, §9).
    The served model id and its release date (GPT-6 Sol was replaced by 6.1 Sol after 7 days), the
    effort actually sent per turn, the harness executable digest and inherited env hash, the local
    arm's weights digest, quant tag, engine and served context (none of 198 popular GGUFs could be
    rebuilt from their cards), the isolation tier, reachable credentials, and the observed network
    capability. Refuse to rank two rows that differ in any of them without saying so. Extends item
    58 and the freeze list from item 31.
70. **A scheduled drift canary with a stated decision rule** (§2). Hermetic Request (constant
    rendered-Request hash), variance-screened items, paired per-item difference with clustered
    standard errors, a control arm and a positive control (deliberately lowered effort), output
    tokens as a second signal, and Livenerf's rule: a 99% interval in 2 consecutive windows and an
    effect of at least 3 points. Restart the baseline whenever the served model id changes.
    Extends item 58.

### Graders

71. **Graders read the Effect log, refuse best-of-k, and survive a hostile agent** (§1, §2, §9).
    Report the distribution over runs, never a selected run (Epoch: one model's gains were "farming
    seed noise"). Hash the grader into the session header and keep the arm label out of the agent's
    view (agents edited verification scripts and disabled a physics engine for a 34,500 times
    speedup). A verified result records the tree digest it checked. Add a fault-injection arm on
    `Effect::Handler::Mock` (lost reply after write, 429, malformed reply) graded by end state, with
    an at-most-once check on the Effect log (two models duplicated an invoice 10 of 10 times after
    a timeout). Count refusals and policy blocks as outcomes. Add "catch" items (impossible,
    planted-fault) with an abstain grader. Extends item 43.
72. **Judges report their calibration; a cheap stand-in must carry a disagreement bound** (§2,
    §10). Brier and ECE with a temperature fitted on a held-out split, for every judge and grader.
    A stand-in sweep (prompted LLM, trained classifier, decision model) on one corpus of approval
    calls, triage verdicts and Rubric items. Before any cheap model replaces an expensive one,
    require a Clopper-Pearson bound on their disagreement plus a permanent audit slice: in
    Jevstiller's test a point-estimate threshold broke a 2% budget in 6 to 12 of 20 splits, the
    bound in 0 to 1. Extends item 44.
73. **Skill and instruction arms get a length-matched placebo and a multiple-comparison
    correction** (§2, §1, §3). 2 of 9 popular skills beat a placebo, 1 did worse, and the placebo
    alone moved cost 2 to 16%. Add a canary-word spec that every instruction source reaches the
    rendered Request, and a per-role "instruction bytes before the first tool call" column. Extends
    item 52.

### Context

74. **One compaction sweep, five strategies, measured on the cache** (§1, §3, §9B, §9C). Arms:
    model-written summary (today's `Context::Compact`); summary plus a findings note (one A/B: the
    arm without the note "folded five times ... never answered"); CliffCompaction-style
    truncate-only, re-derived from the original each time (the prior run's watch item, now arXiv
    `2609.26779` and an MIT repo); state-only rendering (CORVUS's live file registry, SKILL.state);
    and notes plus a fresh window (Codex's `new_context` mode). Trigger axis: none, 25%, 50%,
    agent-authored handoff. Score on success, turns, billed cost and cache-read tokens. Add the
    invariant that the last user instruction survives compaction (a reported failure had it
    vanish). Nobody has measured the cache effect of any of these. Extends items 48 and 49.
75. **Tool-result shape and disclosure** (§2, §3). A bench task whose one decisive failure line
    sits in the tail other harnesses drop, with arms refuse-and-narrow (`Tool::Bounds`),
    head-truncate, digest-and-spill, spill-only. A comment-stripping view arm with a dose-response.
    A distractor-count axis and a masked-description control for tool disclosure (recall fell from
    73.6% to 27.4% as distractors grew). Journal a per-tool description digest for MCP tools and
    flag a mid-session change. Extends items 50 and 57.
76. **Code mode as an arm** (§1). OpenCode 2.0 turns every MCP server into functions in one script
    by default. `eval_ruby` does not exist in `lib/` (verified), so this is build, then bench: round
    trips and tokens per task against per-call tools. M5's code-mode line.
77. **Effort and provider breadth as axes** (§9). Change effort mid-run only through the
    per-message system form, since the top-level form restarts the cache; report cost at matched
    success per effort level (one suite peaked at high, max cost 3 to 4 times high). Per-spawn
    effort and model for subagents, with Haiku 5.5 as a summarizer arm. The OpenAI-compatible
    provider ROADMAP names does not exist (`lib/lain/provider/` has anthropic and ollama,
    verified). Gemini reports input summed across output hops, so context size must be computed,
    not read. Extends items 40 and 56.

### Memory

78. **Memory keyed two ways, graded on uptake and carryover** (§1). Subject-keyed supersession
    against source-digest invalidation over one `Memory::Index`; report "memory used in k of n runs"
    (one system: 8 of 10 vs 0 of 78) and "bad memory carried into a later task". Add an
    agent-authored, unapproved decision as a poisoning fixture. Extends item 61.

### Confinement and the safety seams

79. **DNS and UDP are the open channel in bash confinement** (§6, §7). Checked against
    `planning/specs/bash-confinement.md` on 2026-10-16: the fence limits TCP connects by port and
    blocks abstract sockets and signals outside the sandbox (scenarios at lines 811 and 820), but
    the bwrap stage is `--unshare-pid` with no network namespace (line 979, built that way in `lib/lain/confine/bwrap.rb:51`), and Landlock governs
    TCP only, so a confined command can still resolve names and send UDP. OpenAI's agent reached an
    external chatbot over exactly that: DNS. Arms: TCP-port fence only (the plan), plus
    `--unshare-net`, plus an allowlist resolver on the host that answers only listed names and
    record types. Grade with a canary that resolves a nonce name and opens a raw UDP socket. In the
    same pass, spec that a confined command cannot reach Lain's filesystem sockets (input,
    approval) or journal (a DeepSeek harness CVE let a sandboxed agent set its own session to full
    access), that a call routed through a long-lived server process is still confined (OpenCode
    2.0's background server voided a wrapper sandbox), that the agent cannot rewrite its own
    session file, and that no provider forwards a model-influenced URL. Extends item 59.
80. **Kill the whole tree, and prove it** (§7, §9A). `Shell::Out` and `Shell::Pipeline` signal the
    process group only (verified), and the bash-confinement spec lists "the group kill leaves a
    process alive after a timeout under bwrap" as a risk (line 1129) without a remedy. Use a cgroup v2 kill where available, else a subreaper and pidfd
    scan, and make "could not prove the tree empty" a distinct, journaled outcome. Spec it with a
    double-forked and a `setsid` child. Add a triage rule that `pkill`/`killall -f` with a short
    pattern needs a counted dry run (a postmortem: 144 matches, approved in auto mode). Extends item
    59.
81. **Give the tripwire a measured stop** (§6, §7, §4). OpenAI's monitor alerted in 12 minutes and
    the run was stopped about 2.5 hours later; a detector had excluded the affected environment.
    Record time from ledger hit to stop, add an arm where a hit refuses the next Provider call,
    spec that no environment is excluded from the ledger, and add a registry-recency signal for
    package installs. Observe-first stays. Extends item 59.
82. **A launcher isolation tier with a floor** (§7). Tiers process, container, kernel, VM;
    report the tier in the session header, refuse (never silently downgrade) when the backend
    cannot enforce the requested policy, and give a microVM guest no NIC (Trail of Bits shows the
    emulated NIC is an escape path). Extends item 59 and `references/firecracker-microvm-isolation.md`.
83. **Approval: provenance labels against the judge, on a corpus nobody tuned for** (§4, §7). A
    replay gateway that labels each argument of a state-changing call with the Event it came from
    stopped 98.5% to 99.8% of hijacked calls at 28.9% to 56.7% friction; classifiers stopped 70.2%
    and 26.1%. Run labels, judge, and both. Replay the public 121-case tamper corpus and Lain's own
    journaled `bash` calls, with the corpus's benign cases as the false-positive column. Add the
    approval-binding spec (approve a parked call, mutate its arguments, assert the executed argv is
    the approved one) and a fake-tool simulator for judge tests. Extends items 46 and 63.
84. **A credential broker arm** (§7). Secret in env and masked, secret behind the three-place
    boundary, and secret held by a broker outside confinement that signs per route; scored on a
    prompt-injected agent. Extends item 47 and the secret boundary.

### Orchestration

85. **Fork-and-flush and kill-and-resume as fan-out arms** (§8, §7). Fork 4 children at 33% and
    66% of budget from fresh roots on workspace copies, keep the best branch; the paper
    (`2610.07447`) reports 0.78 against 0.47 single-run and 0.54 best-of-4. Kill K of N children
    at random points and score redo fraction (one report: 449 cut off, 8 resumed, 438 redone).
    Add a notes-to-fresh-instance ablation (a 9B model rose from 45% to 61 to 84% across 6
    generations of inherited notes). Extends items 53 and 55.
86. **The delegation law covers messages, not only spawns** (§8). A message never grants
    capability: a read-only actor cannot cause a write-capable actor's effect that its own path
    would refuse (shutdown-sabotage paper: 38.3% against 8.4% control). Make sibling visibility
    two-sided with a team@k arm (team@5 matched 33 independent agents). Extends items 53 and 54
    and the Mailbox direction.
87. **Measure workspace cost and merge scaling before building** (§8). Lease time and bytes at K=4
    for a reflink or overlay backend; a worker-count sweep reporting the Universal Scalability Law
    fit; a count of branches that pass alone and fail combined; a rule-based worker-health grader
    from signals Lain's leases already carry. Extends items 28 and 60.
88. **Cheap-worker and router arms at equal dollars** (§5, §10). Planner and reviewer on one model,
    worker on a cheaper one, cost split by role, against one strong model. A router arm paired by
    task with intervals on time and pass rate (one design: 62.4% vs 62.8% pass, 0.68 times the time,
    1,242 tasks), noting routers fail open while the approval judge fails closed. Extends item 42.
89. **Cache-hit by provider route, and never-read writes** (§5). Item 56's meter gains two causes:
    a route change between turns, and cache writes no later turn reads (fresh-root children).
    Report per role. Extends item 56.
90. **Unattended-loop levers as arms** (§3). Capped continuation that names open items, a silence
    reminder, an elapsed-over-budget line for fan-out, a random-id delimiter for pasted text; each
    with a control and cache-read accounting. New, under M5.

Lower priority, recorded so they are not lost: a same-task cross-review matrix between two
harnesses (§9B), and a many-agent merge loop with the Lean kernel as an unfakeable grader (§11).

**What is worth researching before it is planned**, as opposed to planned directly: item 79
(network egress and control-plane reach for confined `bash`, which feeds a plan written on
2026-10-16), item 74 (the compaction sweep, whose cache arithmetic nobody has measured), item 83
(provenance labels against the judge, which extends the approval-layering research), and the
first half of item 72 as a short spike (whether a local model reading token probabilities makes a
calibrated judge, which needs logprobs in `Provider::Ollama` first). Items 64 to 66 and 68 are fixes
with known answers.

See `SCOPE.md` for the questions these answer and `planning/hn-agent-landscape-2026-10.md` for the
reconciliation against the plan. `hn-agent-landscape-2026-07.md`, `-2026-08.md`, `-2026-08-14.md`,
`-2026-08-18.md` and `-2026-09-22.md` are the previous windows and are not superseded by this file.
